Note `0` is a rating, not an absence, so the parse only yields `null` when the field is missing. `test/user_rating_test.dart` pins the model, the screen, and that the literal `4.9` is gone from both (it lived in two places, so one revert would restore the lie).
- **انقلابُ سعر الكوادر: تنبيهٌ لا منع، مرآةً لكلِّ جهة (2026-10-04).** Live production data in «فيفا» prices «فيلا أو دور» at 347 ر.س for one crew and **320 for two** — the two chips sit side by side for the client, so she takes two crews for less than one. The price field is free per (type × crews) in **both** editors (`admin_hourly_zones_screen` and `Settings.tsx`) with no comparison in either, so a transposed pair saves and displays with no signal at all. A promotional price is a commercial decision, so the rule **warns and does not block**. It compares each option against the highest price among those with *fewer* crews — one report per offending option, not one per pair — and disabled or zero-priced options are out of scope, since the editor's own text says they are not shown to the client. The rule lives once per side (`lib/utils/crew_price_check.dart`, `admin_panel/src/utils/crewPriceCheck.ts`) with the same cases tested on both, including string keys `'1'..'4'` as the panel's form actually holds them — unconverted, the sort would be lexicographic and «fewer crews» would invert. Note **the warning does not repair existing data**: the live zone is still inverted until someone edits it.

- **سعرٌ مكتوبٌ أنّه شاملُ الضريبة فوق سعرٍ ليس كذلك (2026-10-04).** `hourly_details_screen` printed «الأسعار **شاملة** الضريبة» directly under «اختر نوع سكنك:» and above chips rendering `opt.basePrice` — with the code comment above those chips saying "قبل الضريبة" in as many words, and three other sentences on the same screen agreeing. The same sentence had **already been corrected once in this very file**, and its guard pins that one site only. `test/vat_display_claim_test.dart` sweeps every file in `lib/screens/**` outside `admin/` for eight spellings of the claim in visible text, masks comments and then asserts the old phrase *is* still in the raw text, and pins that the chips still show `basePrice` and not `grossPrice` — if the displayed number ever flips, the sentence must flip with it rather than the guard being edited. Admin screens are excluded on purpose: their stored amounts really are VAT-inclusive.

- **The admin panel's two mirrors**: `admin_panel/src/utils/zoneSchedule.ts` and `serviceMeta.ts` each declare themselves a **mirror** of Flutter code (`admin_zone_schedule_editor.dart`, `service_meta_view.dart`) — nothing but a comment kept them in step, and nothing in TypeScript breaks when the Dart original changes. Both are now tested (`*.test.ts`, 62 tests up from 14). The zone schedule is really a **three-way contract**: two writers (web editor, app editor) and one reader that decides — `zoneDayScheduleForDate` in `index.js`, which requires `Number.isInteger(s) && Number.isInteger(e) && e > s` and otherwise returns `null`, meaning **closed all day**. So an out-of-order range is not rejected with a message: it is silently read as closed — no availability, no booking, no error. The web editor's two hour `<select>`s were independent over 0–23 with no constraint at all, so an admin could save "open 10pm → 8am"; the app editor constrained it but left a hole at the end of the range (start 23 pushes end to `(24).clamp(1,23)` = 23, equal ends, closed again). The rule now lives once per side — `withStart`/`withEnd` in `zoneSchedule.ts`, `hourRangeWithStart`/`End` in `lib/utils/hour_range.dart` — clipping start at 22 and end at 1 so the two can never be equal. Both guards test **every** hour pair (576), not examples, because the hole was in one corner; and both pin the **call sites**, since a correct rule that stops being called leaves the rule's own tests green. `serviceMeta.ts`, checked line by line, had **not** drifted — its tests pin the match rather than fix anything
- **Firestore queries**: do not add composite indexes casually — range/orderBy on one field and filter locally (see `firestore.indexes.json` for what exists). **The one class that genuinely needs them is aggregation:** `sum()`/`average()` require a composite index that includes the **aggregated field**, even when every filter is an equality — the zigzag merge over single-field indexes that serves an ordinary equality query does not serve an aggregation. `Dashboard.tsx` assumed otherwise in its own comment ("مساواة فقط (لا فهرس مركّب)") and the revenue tile sat at `•••` behind «تعذّر تحميل بعض إحصائيات اللوحة» in production, while the `count()` tiles beside it (no aggregated field) were fine. Four indexes cover the three sums, and an `or()` counts as two queries needing one index each. `test/firestore_indexes_guard_test.dart` ties each index to the call site that justifies it in both directions — an index outliving its query is write cost for nothing, and a query whose summed field changes needs a different index. **Indexes now deploy on merge** (`.github/workflows/firestore_indexes_deploy.yml`), and that workflow deploys **`firestore:indexes` only, never `firestore:rules`**: `firestore.rules` carries a STAGE-C warning that its new create rule breaks payments from older app versions until the minimum version is enforced, so publishing rules is a human decision tied to rollout, not a side effect of a merge — the guard forbids both `firestore:rules` and the bare `--only firestore` that would include them. Note a deployed index is not immediately usable: Firestore reports `Building` for minutes to hours on a large collection and the query keeps failing until it finishes
- **Notifications**: operational pushes go through `notification_triggers`; admin broadcasts (`notifications_log`) are marketing unless `operational: true`, and skip users with `notification_prefs.marketing == false`
- **Testability**: models and helpers are pure; screens accept injectable streams/loaders/callbacks (`items`, `loader`, `clock`, `tileLayer`, …) so widget tests run without Firebase. Source-guard tests (`File(...).readAsStringSync()`) pin owner decisions — read the guard before changing what it protects
- **Wallet integrity**: never write to `wallets/*` from the client — Firestore rules block it; all balance changes go through Cloud Functions
- **Three error idioms, and the silent one must still be visible**: `rethrow` (aborting — let the caller show it), `GlobalErrorHandler.handleError` (logs **and** shows the user a snackbar), and `reportSilent` in `lib/utils/error_report.dart` (records to Crashlytics as non-fatal, shows nothing). The third was missing: of 236 `catch` blocks, 49 did nothing but `debugPrint`, which **is not collected or sent** — it looks like handling and is nothing, and `main.dart`'s global handler never sees it either because the error was caught and never rose. So a paid order whose ZATCA invoice failed to generate, or a user whose FCM token failed to save (silently ending their notifications), left no trace anywhere. `reportSilent` is now wired at the sites that touch **money, compliance, or service continuity** — not at all 49: the 13 `fetchZones retry failed` ones have a retry and a fallback, and reporting them would flood Crashlytics, which is worse than its silence. **Empty `catch` blocks are mostly correct here, not sloppy** — most are deliberate, documented compensation (delete an orphaned auth account, then `rethrow` the original error — see `firebase_service.dart`), where swallowing the *cleanup* failure is exactly right. `reason:` must be a **constant** string: Crashlytics groups by it, so `'... for $uid'` yields one group per user and the signal vanishes (that was a real bug in `user_provider`'s own private helper, now consolidated). Variable context goes in `info:`. Direct `recordError` calls are allowed only in `main.dart`, `error_report.dart` and `global_error_handler.dart`; `test/error_visibility_test.dart` pins every critical site by its reason, that reasons stay constant, that the helper never throws and skips web, and that no fourth file calls `recordError` directly
- **`npm audit` in `functions/` reports 8 vulnerabilities and none is exploitable — and `--force` is the real hazard**: the 6 highs are the whole Firebase JS SDK chain reaching `@grpc/grpc-js@1.9.16`, which comes from `@firebase/rules-unit-testing`, a **devDependency** for the emulator rules tests. Production resolves a different copy entirely — `firebase-admin` → `@google-cloud/firestore` → `google-gax` → `@grpc/grpc-js@1.14.5`, outside the advisory range. Note npm hoists the *dev* 1.9.16 to top-level `node_modules/@grpc/grpc-js` and nests the production 1.14.5 under `google-gax`, so checking the bare path reads the wrong one. The 2 moderates (`uuid@9.0.1` via `gaxios@6.7.1` via `@google-cloud/storage@8.2.0`) *are* in the production tree but unreachable: the advisory is `v3`/`v5`/`v6` **with a `buf` argument**, and gaxios calls only `uuid.v4()` (one call, a multipart boundary), while our own code never imports uuid. They are also unfixable here — `@google-cloud/storage@8.2.0` and `firebase-admin@14.5.0` are both the latest published and the former pins `gaxios ^6.0.2`, so the fix is Google's. **Never run `npm audit fix --force` here:** npm's proposed "fix" for the high chain is to downgrade `@firebase/rules-unit-testing` from `^5` to `2.0.7` — three majors back, breaking the rules tests, to patch a dev-only chain. Plain `npm audit fix` is fine and was applied. `functions/test/audit_posture.test.js` pins all of it: the production-resolved grpc-js version, that the package is not downgraded, that it stays in devDependencies, and that our code never imports uuid directly (which would make the advisory reachable)
- **`google_fonts` is pinned to major 8 on purpose, and `go_router` is on 18** — both 9.0.0 and 18.0.0 "migrate to `material_ui` and `cupertino_ui`", the Material/Cupertino libraries being extracted from the SDK into standalone packages. **Flutter 3.47.2 has not finished that move**: `flutter/material.dart` still defines its own `TextTheme` in `src/material/text_theme.dart` (862 lines, a full class) and does not re-export `material_ui`, so one name now has two classes. `google_fonts` 9 is written entirely against the package one, verified against the analyzer rather than inferred: `mui.TextTheme a = GoogleFonts.tajawalTextTheme()` passes while `TextTheme b = ...` fails, and `tajawalTextTheme(const mui.TextTheme())` passes while `tajawalTextTheme(const TextTheme())` fails. **The wall is on the return, not the argument** — building the base as `mui.TextTheme` compiles, then assigning the result to `ThemeData.textTheme` does not, so nothing short of a hand-written 15-field converter gets through. And there is nothing to gain: 9.0.0 is the migration, a higher SDK floor, `GoogleFontsLite` tree-shaking, and a long list of **added fonts** — while we use exactly one font (Tajawal) **already bundled as a local asset**, which `google_fonts` finds in `AssetManifest` before any network fetch. Note 9.0.0 declares `flutter: ">=3.47.0"` and we run 3.47.2, so `pub get` resolves it happily: the constraint does not protect you here, which is the recurring lesson that **`pub get` checks constraints, not classes**. `go_router` 18.0.2 by contrast is a clean upgrade with **zero code changes** — our surface is `GoRoute`/`context.go`/`push`/`pop`/`redirect`/`GoRouterState` and no `ShellRoute` at all, and 18.0.2's two fixes are `ShellRoute`-specific so they do not reach us. `test/google_fonts_pin_test.dart` holds all of this: it pins both majors, pins that we use no `ShellRoute` (adopting one means re-evaluating 18.0.2's semantics fix rather than skipping past it), and — the gate — reads the live SDK to check it still defines `TextTheme`. When a Flutter upgrade makes that check fail, that is the signal to raise `google_fonts` and delete the guard. Its first version counted three parents up from `Platform.resolvedExecutable` (which is `flutter_tester`, not `dart`, under `flutter test`), found nothing, and returned early — **green and hollow**; it now walks up until it finds the marker and fails outright if it cannot, because an inert guard is worse than a broken one.
- **App Check — sending, not enforcing (yet)**: `main.dart` activates App Check (Play Integrity on Android; App Attest with DeviceCheck fallback on Apple, skipped on web for lack of a reCAPTCHA site key), so clients now *send* attestation tokens. **Nothing verifies them**: there is no `enforceAppCheck` in `functions/index.js` and no enforcement in the Firebase Console — and turning either on today is a **service outage, not a hardening**, because the public release 1.2.46 does not carry the package at all, so every request from every current user would be rejected. The gate is: a version carrying App Check has rolled out and the Console's App Check metrics show verified requests near 100%. Two caveats to settle **before** enforcing: `ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift` calls `FirebaseApp.configure()`, which makes iOS silently fall back to DeviceCheck instead of App Attest ([flutterfire#18613](https://github.com/firebase/flutterfire/issues/18613)); and the React admin panel has no App Check, so enforcing on Firestore breaks it too. `test/app_check_guard_test.dart` pins both halves — that tokens are sent, and that enforcement is still off; if it fails because someone enabled enforcement, the question is whether the rollout gate was met, not how to silence the test. Procedure in `production_deployment_guide.md`

## Decisions of the third batch (2026-10-05)

- **Riyadh time has one home — `functions/ksa_time.js`.** Cloud Functions run in UTC (no `TZ` is set anywhere; `timeZone: "Asia/Riyadh"` on `onSchedule` governs *when the cron fires*, not the process). So reading local components off an **absolute instant** returns the UTC hour — Riyadh minus three, and "yesterday" before 03:00. The formula (shift +3h, then read `getUTC*`) was hand-written in **seven** places and **two did not shift**: the two server-side order creators, `verifyMoyasarPayment` building an order from payment metadata (the Apple Pay / native-pay path) and `reconcileOrphanPayments` recovering an orphan payment. Both wrote `booking_time_slot` **three hours early**, and that is not cosmetic: `capacity.js` counts a driver busy *from `booking_time_slot`* for `hours_contracted`, so 11:00–15:00 was blocked while the real 14:00–18:00 stayed free — **a double-booked driver**; the driver dashboard announced the wrong hour; the client reminder reads `d.booking_time_slot ||` a *correct* fallback, so the wrong stored field bypassed the right one; the invoice printed it; and `booking_date` slid a whole day for a 00:00–02:59 booking. **It was a regression a fix introduced:** before `parseKsaIso`, the naive string was parsed as UTC so `getHours()` returned the Riyadh wall-clock numbers *right by accident* while `service_date` was stored three hours late. The instant was fixed and the derivative was not — and `parseKsaIso`'s own comment mentions the derivative without touching it. A distinction the guard depends on: reading local components off a date **built from components** (`new Date(y, m-1, d)` then `getFullYear()`) is correct (same zone out as in) and is still done in three places (contract visit generation ×2, the schedule day sweep) — so the guard pins **the source of the two booking fields**, it does not ban `getDate`. And `3 * 60 * 60 * 1000` is not always a zone offset: in `opsHealthSweep` it is a three-hour grace after the visit ends (next to a 12-hour one), so the guard bans it only inside `new Date(` and then asserts the grace is still there, or it would have nothing to distinguish it from.
- **`payWithWallet` was the fourth payment path that did not reopen a system-cancelled order.** `cancelStaleUnpaidOrders` cancels a pending unpaid order after 30 minutes with `cancel_reason='unpaid_expired'`; verify/webhook/reconcile all merge `reopenFieldsIfSystemCancelled` into the `is_paid` flip, and **a comment describing that defence in detail sat in `index.js` above `_parseKsaIso` with none of the code it described**. Wallet payment left the order `cancelled` and `is_paid: true` — money taken, no service, no refund. **Latent, not live, and saying so matters:** no UI path creates an `orders` doc and then pays it by wallet 30+ minutes later (the summary screen mints a fresh id per attempt, and the wallet path writes the doc seconds before the call) — but `payWithWallet` is a public callable accepting any order id the caller owns, so parity is the guard. `refund_engine.test.js` pinned "three sites" and failed on the fourth: reviewed and updated deliberately, not silenced.
- **Coupon *eligibility* lives in `functions/coupons.js`; the amount was never the whole question.** `_computeTrustedDiscount` exists so a client cannot inflate `discount_amount` — and it checked `status` and `expiry` only, omitting the three the client checks in `order_service.validateCoupon`: `target_user_id` (a referral coupon is addressed to one client, its code is **deterministic** — `REF` + six chars of the uid + `10` — and is pushed in plain text, so another client can spend it), `restricted_zones`, and `uses`/`maxUses` — whose "final prevention is part of server pricing" the `countCouponUseOnOrderCreate` comment promised and server pricing did not have. The order is written from the client's device with `coupon_code` and `discount_amount`, so the path is reachable (tampering, not something an ordinary client hits — the distinction is in the comment). **And rejection alone is not enough:** raising "expected" only flags at `ratio < 0.5`, so an ineligible 10% coupon yields `ratio = 0.9` — no mark, no alert, nothing — which is exactly the referral coupon's rate. So reasons are classified: `ESCALATED_PROBLEMS = {other_user, other_zone}` (what an honest client cannot reach) alerts the admin; the rest are marked `coupon_rejected_reason` without an alert, because a race between apply and pay reaches them in good faith. The client is served either way (Tier A marks and alerts **with** service), so no legitimate order is refused.
- **`promo_codes.expiry` existed in two types, and the server understood one.** The Flutter editor writes `Timestamp`; the web panel wrote the raw `<input type="date">` **string**, and the server's check was `typeof c.expiry.toMillis === "function"` — a string has no `toMillis`, so **the whole check was skipped**: every coupon created in the web panel had no server-side expiry. The Dart client checks both types and blocked it, so the UI was stricter than the server. The panel knew about the mismatch **on read** (a comment says the admin app stores a `Timestamp`) and reproduced it on write. The panel is fixed; `couponExpiryMs` reads both types anyway, because string documents exist in production — fixing the writer is not enough. Related: `showDatePicker` returns **midnight**, and `expiry` is compared `< now`, so a card reading "expires 2026-12-31" was dead on the morning of the 31st and "expires today" was dead at creation. Both editors now store the **last instant of the chosen day** (`endOfDayLocal` / `endOfLocalDay`); the fix is in the writers, so existing documents keep their instant.
- **An invoice's issue instant has one source — `lib/utils/invoice_stamp.dart`.** Five of six sites building the ZATCA QR passed `timestamp: DateTime.now()` — the moment the **file** was generated — and `zyiarah_pdf_service` printed "التاريخ / Date" from `DateTime.now()` too. On the happy path the gap is seconds, but the invoice has **two implemented regeneration paths** (`invoice_pdf_status: 'retrying'`): the client's button on the success screen and the admin's in the invoice log. Regenerating an invoice that failed days earlier printed **today's date** on a simplified tax invoice for an order paid days before, and its QR (Tag 3 — the supply date ZATCA reads) carried the same wrong value. In the admin screen the `InvoiceView` loading the row **already held** the right `issuedAt` and `DateTime.now()` was passed next to it. And `InvoiceView.qrData()`'s comment claims it is "the same code printed in the PDF" — so one invoice carried **two different QR codes** and the comment denied it. `invoiceQrFor` is now the single gate to `generateZatcaQrCode` from `lib/`, `generateAndUploadInvoice` requires `issuedAt` and prints it, the two retry paths derive it from the document, and the three immediate-issue sites capture `DateTime.now()` **once** for both the print and the code.
- **Account deletion stranded the wallet balance, and its dialog promised what does not happen.** `processAccountDeletion` deletes the Auth user, `users/{uid}` and the FCM tokens — and **nothing in it looked at `wallets/{uid}`**. The auth identity goes and the balance stays in a document keyed by a uid nobody can ever sign in with again: the client's money held forever, with no notice and no record of the debt anywhere; the profile screen, which *has* the balance loaded for the card that displays it, did not mention it in the confirmation dialog. Now: read the wallet **before** deleting auth (order is the check — a read afterwards prevents nothing), record `wallet_balance_at_deletion` on `account_deletions/{uid}` where humans read it, mark the wallet `owner_deleted`, and alert `super_admin`/`accountant_admin`. **We do not zero the wallet** — zeroing destroys the evidence of the debt — and **deletion still proceeds**, because the right to delete is an Apple requirement and is not withheld over a debt the business owes; the wallet read sits in its own `try` and a failure is logged, never thrown. The dialog said "all your data, **invoices** and past services will be permanently erased" and "right to be forgotten: **all** your tracking records will be deleted" — both untrue: tax invoices and orders are retained under the VAT regulation and `processAccountDeletion` never touches `orders`, which keep `client_name`/`client_phone`/`client_email` as recorded. **The first draft of this fix replaced the lie with another one** ("the rest is anonymised") — nothing in the repository anonymises an order; the guard now bans the word until something justifies it.
- **`lib/utils/qatrat.dart`: the points rate once, and the number shown comes from the deposit.** `50` was hand-written in five places (the server minimum, the server's division, the button's condition, the round-down, the progress bar) — the VAT story's shape, so unification is openly **preventive**. What was not preventive: `redeemQatratPoints` returns `{success, newBalance, newPoints}`, the Dart service returned a `bool` and dropped both, and the screen recomputed the amount itself (`earned = toRedeem / 50.0`) and showed it. So "redeemed 100 points for 2.00 SAR" was a figure that never came from the deposit — and if the rate ever diverged she would be told a number that was not credited. The screen now shows the difference the server actually made, and says success without a figure when the number is absent (an older server), the same shape as "—" elsewhere on the card.
- **An operational broadcast could not be sent from the web panel at all.** `isMarketingBroadcast(data)` is `data.operational !== true`, so **every broadcast is marketing by default** and `_deliverBroadcast` drops everyone who turned off "offers and marketing", push and in-app box alike. The Flutter editor has an explicit switch for this; `Notifications.tsx` **never wrote the field**. A maintenance, outage or schedule-change notice sent from the panel was withheld from every client who opted out — and the panel showed "sent successfully" with an ordinary-looking history row. The panel now carries the same switch with the Flutter editor's wording **verbatim** (the difference in wording is a decision, not taste) and resets it after sending, or "operational" would stick to the next broadcast. The guard pins **the set of writers** of `notifications_log` plus that each one writes the field — and it found a third writer I had not counted (`zyiarah_messaging_service`, the scheduling path), which passes `operational` through from the screen rather than relying on its own default, so it is not a hole.
- **The web panel could not put a product in the companies store.** `products.store_audience` ∈ {`client`, `companies`} and absence is `client` for backward compatibility (`store_service.dart`) — a default meant for **old** documents, not for one created today. `StoreProducts.tsx` wrote `{...formData}` and `formData` had no such field, so every product created there landed in the client store silently and there was no way to reach the companies store; editing a companies product from the panel preserved the field (`updateDoc` only touches what it is given — checked before claiming it) but the admin could not see which store it belonged to or move it. Fixed with the Flutter editor's own picker and a row badge.
- **A driver nobody has rated showed "5.0 ★" — and was ranked as if best.** Both provisioning paths seed `rating: 5.0` on the driver document **with no counter**, and `aggregateDriverRating` excludes that seed from the average explicitly (`count > 0 ? ... : 0`) — so the server itself knows 5.0 is not a rating. Two surfaces displayed it as earned: `admin_staff_performance_screen` defaulted `rating_avg ?? 5.0` and then **sorted** by rating × completed, so a driver with 17 completed and no ratings (85) outranked one with 20 and a real 4.2 average (84) and the "top performer" card could name someone nobody rated — the display was not the only thing wrong, the ranking an admin acts on was; and `Drivers.tsx` printed `{driver.rating}` raw. This is the client-side "تقييمك 4.9 ★" defect on the driver side, with one difference: it becomes real after the first rating, so **the discriminator is `rating_count`, not the presence of `rating`**. Unrated now reads "—" and sorts after the rated by completed count alone. The seed itself is left: documented, server-excluded, and harmless once both surfaces read the counter. A third site looked identical and is **not** a defect: `admin_insights._buildReputationSentinel` reads `rating ?? 5.0` but on **orders**, gated by `rating_comment != null`, so the default cannot pull an unrated order into the low-rating list — left as is, with the guard pinning the comment condition because losing it would make it a real defect.
- **A dead free-order branch that would have produced an order nothing dispatches.** `payment_summary_screen` carried a `'subscription'` payment branch creating a free order (`isFree: true` ⇒ `amount: 0`, `is_paid: false`), and `_selectedPaymentMethod` is **never set to it** (the offered options are card/tamara/stc_pay/wallet; Apple/Google Pay call the handler themselves). Nothing saw it: `no_dead_code_test` covers `lib/services/` and `lib/utils/`, **not the screens** — a gap worth naming. Had it ever been wired it would have been a compound silent defect, because four server paths require payment: `onOrderWritten` gates assignment on the `is_paid` flip (**no driver, ever**), `capacity.countBookings` skips `is_paid !== true` (consumes no capacity, so the day is sold twice), `remindClientsUpcomingAppointments` requires paid-or-assigned (no reminder), and `cancelStaleUnpaidOrders` exempts only Tamara/Tabby and a Moyasar payment id (**cancelled after 30 minutes** as `unpaid_expired`). Real subscription visits never pass through the screen at all: `activateContractOnPaid` generates them server-side with `is_paid: true`, so they are assigned, counted and reminded like any paid order — deleting was right, not wiring. The guard pins the deletion **and its reason** (the four conditions, and that the comment carrying the reason survives), and bans exempting `subscription` from the stale sweep, since that would hide the defect rather than fix it.
- **One pattern keeps recurring, so name it: a decision field read with a silent default, known to only one editor.** Four instances now — the force-update build keys (2026-10-04), `show_in_offers`, `operational`, `store_audience`. Three of them in one batch. The shape is always: a reader defaults a missing field to something plausible, one editor exposes the decision, the other does not write the field at all, and the result is a quiet wrong with a successful-looking UI. So the guards here compare **the whole set of fields each editor writes** (as `amounts.test.js` and `app_update_keys_test` do) rather than asserting one field exists.
- **Two method notes.** (1) A guard that strips comments and then scans for a term **fails on its own documentation** — it happened three more times in this batch (`timestamp: DateTime.now()`, the word «يُجهَّل», `/ 50.0`), so each now strips comment lines *and then asserts the term is still in the raw text*, so over-stripping cannot hollow the check. (2) **Extracting a written field set by matching `'key':` is blind to a key written as a constant** — the first zone-document diff reported a divergence that did not exist, because `admin_hourly_zones_screen` writes `kEventWorkerHourPriceField:`. Resolve `const String kX = 'y';` before comparing. Both the zone document (22 fields each side) and the two package catalogs (`subscription_packages`, `event_worker_packages`) were then confirmed **identical** — those mirrors hold and their comments are accurate.
- **Still open / owner-only.** The server-side fixes (Riyadh time, coupon eligibility and expiry, account deletion) do not take effect until `functions` is deployed, and the panel fixes until the panel is deployed; both deploy automatically on `main` unless the merge message carries the skip marker, in which case run the two workflows by hand. `firestore.rules` was not touched in this batch. `Timestamp.now()` vs `serverTimestamp()` is still mixed in the panel (`cancelled_at`, `completed_at`, `processed_at` come from the admin's browser clock) — noted, not changed, because each is a display/audit field rather than a money comparison.

## Decisions of the fourth batch (2026-10-05)

- **«هل الطلبُ ما زال مفتوحاً؟» كان تعداداً، فسَقطت منه كلُّ حالةٍ أُضيفت بعده — `lib/utils/order_activity.dart` + `admin_panel/src/utils/orderActivity.ts`.** The same split (active / finished) was hand-written in **three** screens, and all three wrote it as an enumeration of the *active* statuses — so every status added later fell out silently. It was correct in one and wrong in two. `admin_insights_screen` («طلبات نشطة», the owner's own tile) counted three statuses from `orders` — `pending`/`assigned`/`in_progress` — so `scheduled`, `accepted` and **`on_the_way`** were not active: a driver on his way to the client did not appear in the number. From `store_orders` it counted `pending`/`processing`, and **nothing writes either** on a store order — the real cycle is `awaiting_payment ⇒ under_review ⇒ delivering ⇒ delivered` (owner decision «لا موافقة قبل الدفع», in `store_service.dart`) — so the store's share of that counter was **zero always, structurally**. `Dashboard.tsx` showed «طلبات بانتظار الموافقة» from `where('status','==','pending')` on `store_orders`: **zero forever**, under a title naming an approval step that was removed; it now counts what actually awaits a click (`under_review` → «جاري التوصيل», `delivering` → «تم التسليم», the two buttons the admin screens really show) and is titled «طلبات متجر تحتاج إجراء». `orders_list_screen` was the correct one — fixed once when a paid order vanished from both tabs — and `kTerminalOrderStatuses` is taken **verbatim** from its `historyStatuses`; but two enumerations left a latent hole there too, since a status in neither list (`out_for_delivery`, `preparing` — both in `notifyClientOnStoreOrderStatus`'s map) drops out of **both tabs**. The rule is now "finished is a short fixed set, everything else is open", so tomorrow's status cannot fall out. `refunded` is deliberately **not** in the set: it is a `payment_status` value, never a `status` one, and adding it would introduce a value nothing writes — the very defect being fixed. One judgement stated openly: "open" includes pre-payment (`awaiting_payment`), which preserves the existing intent (the code already counted `pending` and `waiting_payment`); "paid work in progress only" is one constant away. `test/order_activity_test.dart` pins the rule, the **cross-language set equality** by reading both files, that the set the test reads is the set the code uses, every call site (a rule not called is a dead rule), and that a store order is still created `awaiting_payment` — if that changes the rule needs review, not silencing.

- **لوحةُ الويبِ كانت تَبتلعُ فشلَ أربعِ كتاباتٍ إداريّة.** `console.error` is not a notification, and the click ends with nothing visible because the row does not change (no write ⇒ no listener update) — so the admin assumes he mis-clicked and tries again. `Orders.tsx` toasts at all five of its sites, so the convention existed and four places broke it: **`StoreOrders`** («جاري التوصيل»/«تم التسليم» — and the client is not notified either, since the push is triggered by the status change), **`Support`** (a reply to a ticket: the admin thinks the client was answered and she was not — the wording is «تعذّر **إكمال** إرسال الرد» on purpose, because the message is written before the ticket is marked `replied`, so the second write can fail alone and «لم يُرسَل» would be a lie; it also warns against a blind retry, which would double-post to the client), **`StoreProducts`** (saving a product: the modal stays open — closing it is the last line of the `try` — with no reason shown), and **`Marketing`** (toggling a coupon: a coupon that looks disabled is still accepted; deleting in the same file did toast). The deliberately silent ones stay silent and the guard pins that too: the audit log is best-effort (its failure must not make successful work look failed — same rule as `lib/services/audit_service.dart`), and a failed settings *read* is shown as a failure state in the page itself, not a toast. `test/panel_write_failure_test.dart` pins the four and the two, then re-runs the sweep that found them and compares the **whole set** against the allowlist, so a fifth site fails instead of passing.

- **مسارا الدفعِ اختلفا في التحقّقِ من السعر — `functions/price_verify.js`.** `verifyMoyasarPayment` resolves the order's zone by `zone_id` then by name, subtracts the **trusted** discount before comparing, and flags `price_unverifiable` + alerts the admins when a priceable kind cannot be priced — "يَسدّ ثغرةَ إسقاطِ `zone_name` للتهرّبِ من التحقّق" in its own words. `payWithWallet` read `zone_name` **only**, and if it was absent **skipped verification entirely with no flag and no alert**: the same evasion, open on the other path. Three divergences exactly, two of them live. (1) `zone_id` was not read on the wallet path — **latent**: nothing in the repository writes `zone_id` onto an order (the key appears only in audit details), so the name is what resolves today on both paths. (2) No `price_unverifiable` and no alert on the wallet path — **live**. (3) The trusted discount was not subtracted on the wallet path — **live, and it hurts the honest client**: a percentage coupon above 50% drives `ratio < 0.5` so a perfectly good payment is flagged `price_mismatch` and the admins are alerted about it, and above 80% it falls under the rejection threshold so a **valid payment is refused** with «المبلغ لا يطابق السعر المعتمد لمنطقتك». A fourth, found while writing the module: `_isPriceableKind` enumerated four kinds by hand and omitted **`event_workers`**, which `pricing.js` really does price — so an event-workers order with no `zone_name` evaded verification silently on **both** paths, which is exactly what that predicate exists to prevent. The list now lives in `pricing.js` as `PRICEABLE_KINDS`, and `functions/test/price_verify.test.js` compares **every `kind ===` branch** in `computeExpectedBasePrice` against it (matching `kind` only when not preceded by a dot — `p.kind === "rug"` inside the sofa branch is a *piece* kind and the first version of the check caught it), so a sixth kind fails instead of evading. The module takes `db` as its first argument and is tested against a ~40-line fake (the `refund_engine.js` rule); what is deliberately **not** shared is the *policy* — Moyasar alerts immediately and enforces Tier B, the wallet flags silently and leans on `opsHealthSweep`, which its own comment states. The guard also pins that `index.js` holds **no** hand-written `.where("name", "==", od.zone_name)` any more, since that is how the two paths drifted apart in the first place.

- **حارسٌ يَسقطُ على توثيقِه: مرّتان أُخريان (المجموع ستٌّ هذه الجلسة).** `historyStatuses` named in the comment explaining its removal, and `price_unverifiable` named in `_flagZoneGeoMismatch`'s header (so the count read 3, not 2). Both fixed the same way and it is now reflex: strip whole comment lines, **then counter-assert the term is still present in the raw text**, so over-stripping cannot hollow the guard out. And `tsc` caught a mistake of mine that no test would have: `StoreProducts` destructured `confirm` alone from the notification hook, so `toast` was undefined.

- **والقسمةُ نفسُها نسخةٌ خامسةً عند السائق — `lib/models/driver_schedule.dart`.** `DriverSchedule.activeStatuses` repeated `kActiveAssignedStatuses` letter for letter, and `historyStatuses` was `{completed, cancelled}`. Two consequences, both of the same family. **The active list must stay a positive enumeration** and that is not a defect: it feeds a Firestore `whereIn`, and Firestore has no "not in this set", so the negative rule (`orderIsOpen`) cannot serve there — it is now *derived* from `kActiveAssignedStatuses` instead of retyped, so a status added to the lifecycle tomorrow cannot silently drop a job off a driver's phone. **The history filter is a rule now** (`isHistory` = not active), because an order carrying his `driver_id` in a status outside both lists appeared in **neither** view: the clearest case is an order reopened to `pending` after a late payment confirms (`reopenFieldsIfSystemCancelled`, which keeps `driver_id`) — assigned to him in the admin's eyes, invisible on his device. The task card already had a `default` branch rendering «معلقة» with an hourglass: UI written for a state the filter never let through, the same shape as the three unreachable error screens found earlier in the session. Pinned in `test/driver_schedule_test.dart`, including that the active query stays a positive `whereIn` so the fix cannot be "simplified" into breaking it.

- **«لا رقمَ قبل أن نعرفه» عضوٌ رابعٌ، وأخطرُها: تحذيرُ رصيدِ المحفظةِ قبلَ حذفِ الحساب — `lib/utils/wallet_deletion_notice.dart`.** The delete-account dialog warns that a remaining wallet balance is locked forever (the uid can no longer sign in), and the condition was `!_walletError && _walletBalance > 0`. So the warning disappeared in the two cases where it matters most: **before the wallet finished loading** (`_walletBalance` starts at `0.0`, so the condition is false — a client who opens the dialog during the read deletes her account never told about her money) and **when the read failed** (the condition silences it explicitly, under a comment saying «القرارُ قرارُها» — and she cannot decide what she is not told). The three earlier members of this family displayed a *wrong number*; this one **omits a warning about money at the one irreversible moment**. The correct shape was in the same file all along: every other display site checks `_walletLoaded` **and** `_walletError`, and the qatrat redeem chip right above was corrected for exactly this mistake earlier. The rule is now a pure three-way state (`none` / `balance` / `unknown`) with **ignorance warning rather than staying silent**, and the unknown text is conditional («إن كان فيها رصيد») and carries no figure. A negative balance is `none` on purpose — a debt is not money she loses. `test/wallet_deletion_notice_test.dart` pins all three states, that the old condition is gone from the code but still present in the comment explaining its removal, and that the unknown branch never interpolates the balance.

- **صفُّ بطاقاتِ «الطلبات» في لوحةِ المالكِ كان ثلاثةَ أرقامٍ لا تُقابلُ عناوينَها (`admin_insights_screen`).** The same family as the «طلبات نشطة» counter, three tiles side by side. **«الطلبات المجدولة»** counted `pending || waiting_payment` — and `waiting_payment` on its own is written **nowhere** (the live name is `awaiting_payment`; `approved_waiting_payment` is a contract state) — while `scheduled`, the word in the title, was not counted at all. **«طلبات المتجر»** counted `pending`, which nothing writes on a store order: **zero always**, the Dart twin of the panel tile. **«عقود تنفيذية»** had `count: 0` written by hand in the source — a number nobody computed, the `rating 4.9` shape again. All three now count *what awaits the owner's action* and each title says what it counts: pre-dispatch orders **with no driver** (`kPreDispatchStatuses`, matching `approveAndAssignOrder`'s domain exactly), `storeOrderNeedsAction`, and contracts in `pending` («قيد المراجعة» in the contracts screen, the only contract state waiting on an admin). The contracts figure is a server-side `count()` with one equality filter, so it needs no composite index — unlike `sum()`, which does even then (see the Firestore-queries bullet); the guard pins that it stays a `count()` with no `orderBy`. Note the maintenance loops in the same method run over a list that is **deliberately always empty** (`_maintenance = []`, maintenance was removed at the root), so rewiring them changed no displayed number — said plainly rather than counted as a fix.

- **آثارُ التقييمِ خادميّةٌ وحدَها، وما كان في العميلِ ميتٌ بثلاثةِ وجوهٍ (2026-10-05).** `submitOrderRating` did three things after writing the rating, none of which worked. (1) It called `alertReputationRisk` (an email with the photo and the comment) and `notifyAdminOfLowRating` for a rating ≤ 2 — both written **by the client** to `ADMIN_BROADCAST`, which the impersonation guard in `index.js` refuses in as many words («وأي بثٍّ من عميل يُرفَض»), stamping the trigger `refused_untrusted_sender`. So every low rating left a refused trigger document and nothing arrived; the replacement already existed server-side as `exports.notifyAdminOnLowRating`, whose own comment names this blocked path. (2) It aggregated the driver's average client-side, which the rules always deny (`drivers` write is `isOrdersManager`) — and **had it ever succeeded it would have corrupted the number**, because it seeds the average with `5.0` when absent, exactly what the server refuses to do («البذرُ الثابت لا يَدخلُ المتوسط»), and would then double-count against `exports.aggregateDriverRating`. (3) A comment asserting «لا يوجد Cloud Function يُجمِّع التقييمات حالياً — مطلوبةٌ لاحقاً (backlog)», while that function has existed and works; a reader acting on it would widen the rules and reinstate the double count. All three are gone, the two now-callerless service methods with them (which `no_dead_code_test` would otherwise have flagged), and the comment now names the two server functions. The client writes `rating` on the order and nothing else — which is what actually happened before the cleanup too, so **no behaviour changed**; what changed is that the code no longer claims otherwise. Note the scope trap: `order_service` touches `drivers` legitimately in `updateOrderStatus` (the driver's own availability), so the guard is scoped to `submitOrderRating`'s body — the first version banned the collection file-wide and failed on honest code.

- **`functions_delete_once.yml` يَحذفُ أربعاً، والميّتُ ستٌّ.** The one-shot workflow deletes `checkHourlySlotAvailability`, `findNearestDrivers`, `generateSubscriptionVisits` and `manualSendNotification`, and all four are still clientless (the two `generateSubscriptionVisits` hits in `contract_signing_screen` are **comments** naming the fields it reads, not calls). But two more are dead and **outside that list**: `sendNotificationToAdminsOnNewMaintenance` and `notifyClientOnMaintenanceRejected`. They are Firestore triggers, so `callable_names_test`'s clientless check cannot see them — a trigger dies the other way, by its collection having no writer, and `maintenance_requests` has none: the service was removed at the root and only reads remain (admin search, the order-details fallback for old records). Since the workflow is a *one-shot* that deletes itself after running, running it as it stands leaves those two published with no mechanism left. The gap is now pinned in `test/callable_names_test.dart` rather than left to memory: the two names must still be exported, and `maintenance_requests` must still have no writer — if a writer returns the triggers are alive again, and the test says to take them off the list rather than delete them from production. Adding names to a destructive workflow is the owner's call, so the list was left as it is.

- **ملفٌّ لا يَستورِدُه أحدٌ في اللوحةِ لا يُحذّرُ منه TypeScript — و`src/types/index.ts` كان خريطةً خاطئةً لنموذجِ البيانات (2026-10-05).** The Dart side has `no_dead_files_test` for exactly this; the panel had nothing, and a sweep found one non-test, non-entry file nothing imports: **103 lines of leftover scaffold from a generic ride-hailing template**. It was not merely redundant, it was wrong in every direction that matters: `Order` with `pickupLocation`/`dropoffLocation`/`driverId` and `paymentMethod: 'cash' | 'card' | 'wallet'` — and **cash on delivery was removed at the root by owner decision** (`no_cod_test` guards it in `lib/`); `OrderStatus` spelling `in-progress` with a hyphen while the real value is `in_progress`, and missing every status that exists today; `Ticket.status: 'open' | 'in-progress' | 'closed'` against the real `open`/`replied`/`resolved`; `Driver.status: 'online' | 'offline' | 'busy' | 'suspended'` against `available`/`en_route`/`in_service`; `AdminUser.role: 'Super Admin' | 'Accountant' | …` against the four real role ids; `Coupon` with `maxUsage`/`expiryDate` against `maxUses`/`expiry`. The trap is that the interfaces are **self-consistent**, so a new page built on them compiles clean with wrong field names and wrong status literals and fails only against a real document. Deleted; every page already declares its own shape (`Support.tsx` has its own `Ticket`). `admin_panel/src/no_dead_files.test.ts` is the standing guard — `main.tsx` and `*.test.ts*` are the only exemptions, it asserts it actually read files (an inert guard is worse than none), and it additionally forbids `'in-progress'` returning anywhere. Enumerated with `import.meta.glob`, not `node:fs`, per the rule that has already tripped two guards here.

- **العميلةُ كانت تُخاطَبُ بالمذكَّرِ في ٧٦ موضعاً — `test/feminine_address_test.dart`.** The server-side copy in `functions/index.js` has addressed her as female from the start («دفعتكِ مؤكّدة»، «طلبكِ»، «نتمنى لكِ تجربةً سعيدة»), and so do a few client strings («استخدميها أو تواصلي مع الدعم»، «لن تتمكّني»); the rest of the client UI did not. **The distinction that makes this tractable is grammatical, and it is deliberate:** an unvocalized attached pronoun — «طلبك»، «حسابك»، «رصيدك»، «محفظتك» — reads correctly for either gender in written Arabic, so it is *not* an error and was **not touched**; what has no feminine reading at all is the **verb** («اختر» cannot address a woman under any vocalization) and the **adjective** («متأكد»). So the sweep covers imperatives and adjectives only — `اختر`→`اختاري`, `أدخل`→`أدخلي`, `اضغط`→`اضغطي`, `حاول`→`حاولي`, `تحقّق`→`تحقّقي`, `تواصل`→`تواصلي`, `أعد المحاولة`→`أعيدي المحاولة`, `تأكد`→`تأكّدي`, `هل أنت متأكد`→`هل أنتِ متأكدة` — 70 lines across 18 client screens plus six shared sites that only she reads (`support_fab`, `rating_dialog`'s `selectReasonHint`, `tapToTrackMap`/`tapToViewDetails`, and the two client-facing exception texts in `order_service`/`moyasar_service`). **The driver and the admin are exempt on purpose** and the guard pins that too: the driver is male in this business (14 masculine sites in his screens) and the admin is the owner (45), so feminising either is an error, not a fix. Two traps worth keeping: the pattern must not match «التحقق», a *verbal noun* («جاري التحقق من التوفر») — the first version did, which would have produced «التحقّقي»; and the «و» of «وأعد المحاولة» is a conjunction prefix, not a word letter, so it needs its own rule (12 sites). One guard elsewhere pinned a rewritten literal (`offers_screen_coupons_test`) and was repointed, not weakened. And the first version of the exemption check used a **count threshold** («more than 5» against 14), so feminising eight driver strings passed green — proven by a bite test; it now pins named sentences instead, which is the same lesson as every other count-based guard here.

- **حارسُ «لا نصَّ استثناءٍ خامّاً» كان على خمسِ شاشاتٍ والقاعدةُ عامّة — فوُجد ١٣ موضعاً في وجه العميلة (2026-10-05).** `net_timeout_guard_test` holds two rules; its **timeout** rule was widened to all 41 screens when the fault turned out to be general, and its **raw-exception-text** rule was left at the original five. Widening it found 13 more sites across 11 client screens, the worst being `store_payment_screen`'s «خطأ في بوابة تمارا: $e» — where `$e` is `Exception('فشل الاتصال ببوابة التقسيط: …')`, so the client read **«خطأ في بوابة تمارا: Exception: فشل الاتصال ببوابة التقسيط: يجب تسجيل الدخول أولاً»**: two stacked prefixes, the Latin word `Exception` inside an Arabic snackbar, and a cause that is simply false (an auth error announced as a connection failure). All 13 now show a human Arabic line and keep the diagnosis in `debugPrint`/`reportSilent`. **The admin and driver screens are exempt on purpose, and the guard pins that too** (62 sites): raw exception text is diagnosis the owner wants — `admin_order_details` prints «خطأ غير متوقع: $e» deliberately — so hiding it there is a loss, not a fix. Two mechanical notes: the window must be the snackbar's **balanced parens**, not a 450-character slice — the slice reached past the end of a `const SnackBar` in `driver_dashboard` and flagged a `debugPrint` in the *next* branch, a pure false positive; and paren *balancing* is not the non-greedy regex that made the first version of this check inert, since it counts opens and closes and so passes over the parens inside `toString()`. Removing `$e` made those strings constant, which `prefer_const_constructors` then flagged at 14 sites — fixed, not silenced. Server side, exactly one client-facing message was masculine and un-actionable: `createTamaraCheckout`'s «فشل إنشاء جلسة الدفع — تحقق من بيانات الطلب» told her to inspect data she cannot see, and a sibling message showed her the word «السيرفر»; the other 15 masculine server strings are admin- or driver-facing and correct in place.

- **«حارسٌ ضيّقٌ وقاعدةٌ عامّة» نمطٌ يُستحسنُ مسحُه بنفسِه — و`no_dead_code_test` كان ثالثَه.** After the raw-exception rule turned out to be scoped to five screens while the fault was general, a sweep of every guard carrying a hard-coded file list found `no_dead_code_test` limited to `lib/services` and `lib/utils` although its rule ("every method and getter must be named from somewhere else") is general. Pointing it at `lib/models` and `lib/providers` found **three dead getters** — zero in models, three in providers — and each needed the question this file keeps asking, *was the wiring forgotten or was it written and never used?*, answered differently. **`UserProvider.profileError`: forgotten.** Its own doc comment says «آخر خطأ في تحميل الملف الشخصي — **تعرضه الواجهة** بدل إسقاط الجلسة صامتاً», and nothing displayed it: a failed profile load ends `isLoading`, leaves `user` null, and the dashboard renders with no name and a tracking card with no uid, **saying nothing** — the stated half of an earlier fix (never sign the user out over a data failure) shipped, the telling half did not. It is now a banner on `client_dashboard` whose text says the session is intact (so she does not read it as being logged out) with a retry that calls `refreshUser`, the recovery path itself. **`OrderProvider.trackingOrders`: never used** — the tracking screen queries Firestore by order id, and this was a sixth hand-written status enumeration; deleted. **`ConfigProvider.checkoutVariantName`: never used** — an A/B variant name read from Firestore and stored, while a variant name exists only to be attached to a conversion measurement and there is none; deleted with its assignment (the button colour beside it is live). The scope is now `services + utils + models + providers`; **screens stay out deliberately**, since they carry `build`/`initState` overrides and callbacks invoked from the widget tree rather than from code, and would need a different filter.

- **«لا رجعةَ في هذا الإجراء» — ولا كلمةَ عمّا يَحدثُ للمال (2026-10-05).** حوارُ تأكيدِ إلغاءِ الطلبِ في `orders_list_screen` كان يَقولُ سطراً واحداً: «هل أنتِ متأكدة… لا يمكن التراجع عن هذا الإجراء». والنتيجةُ الخادميّةُ **ثلاثٌ** مختلفةٌ اختلافاً جوهريّاً، والزرُّ نفسُه يَظهرُ في الثلاثِ (`pending` و`awaiting_payment`، وهما ما تُجيزُه القواعدُ للعميلة): **غيرُ مدفوعٍ** ⇒ لا شيء؛ **زيارةُ باقةٍ** (`payment_method == 'subscription'`) ⇒ `onOrderRewards` يَستثنيها من إيداعِ المحفظةِ صراحةً و`settleVisitAccounting` لا يَردُّ زيارةً لم تُستهلَك (`never_counted`)، فلا مبلغَ ولا حركةَ عدّادٍ والزيارةُ باقيةٌ في رصيدِها؛ **مدفوعٌ بغيرِ ذلك** ⇒ `creditCancelledRefund` يُودِعُ المبلغَ **كاملاً في محفظةِ زيارة، لا إلى البطاقة**. والفرقُ الأخيرُ ليس تفصيلاً: **لا مسارَ سحبٍ نقديٍّ للمحفظةِ في المستودعِ كلِّه** — لا في التطبيقِ ولا في الخادم — فرصيدُها يُنفَقُ داخلَ التطبيقِ وحدَه، وعميلةٌ دفعت ببطاقةٍ تَنتظرُ استرداداً إليها. وهي عائلةُ `wallet_deletion_notice` بعينِها: **تحذيرٌ عن مالٍ غائبٌ عن اللحظةِ التي لا رجعةَ فيها**، وقاعدتُها المقرَّرةُ هناك تَسري حرفاً («عندَ الجهلِ نُخبِرُ بلا رقم»): مستندٌ قديمٌ بلا `amount` — أو بصفرٍ، وهو ما يَجعلُ الخادمَ يَتخطّى الإيداعَ أصلاً — يُنتجُ جملةً شرطيّةً لا رقماً مُلفَّقاً.

  **والسطحُ الثاني هو نصفُ العطل، لا زيادةً عليه.** إلغاءُ الإدارةِ ليس حواراً بل **قائمةٌ منسدلةٌ وزرُّ حفظ**: تَحويلُ الحالةِ إلى «ملغي» يُودِعُ المبلغَ في محفظةِ العميلةِ تلقائيّاً بلا أن يُقال، في الشاشةِ نفسِها التي تَحملُ بطاقةَ عمليّاتِ ميسر (إلغاءُ التفويضِ/الاسترداد) — أي أنّ الخيارَ الآخرَ على بُعدِ تمريرةٍ والقرارُ يُتَّخَذُ عنه بالصمت. فـ«قاعدةٌ عامّةٌ مُنفَّذةٌ في سطحٍ واحد» هي النمطُ المتكرّرُ هنا، والقاعدةُ تَسكنُ مرّةً في `lib/utils/cancel_refund_notice.dart` بثلاثِ حالاتٍ نقيّةٍ ونصَّينِ يَتبعانِ الجمهورَ (العميلةُ مؤنّثاً، والمالكُ بوصفِ ما يَجري للعميلةِ ومعه إشارةٌ إلى بطاقةِ ميسر) — كما في بقيّةِ المشروع: القرارُ واحدٌ والصياغةُ تَختلف. وتأكيدُ الإدارةِ **قبلَ** `setState` وقبلَ النداء، وتراجُعُه يُوقِفُ الحفظَ فعلاً — `test/cancel_refund_notice_test.dart` (١٣ فحصاً) يَشدُّ ذلك الترتيبَ لأنّ تأكيداً بعدَ الإلغاءِ لا يَمنعُ شيئاً، ويَشدُّ القاعدةَ بشواهدِها الخادميّةِ الثلاثِ (الاستثناءُ، و`never_counted`، و`increment(amount)`) فزوالُ أيٍّ منها يَجعلُ جملةً من الثلاثِ كذباً فتُراجَع، وأنّ الشاشتَين **تُناديانِ** القاعدةَ ولا تُعيدانِ تعدادَ الاشتراكِ بجوارِ النداء، وأنّ لا `exports.*[Ww]ithdraw` ظهرَ (فظهورُه يَنقضُ جوهرَ الرسالة). سبعةُ اختباراتِ قضمٍ، كلٌّ وحدَه.

  **ومرفوعٌ إلى المالكِ لا مُغيَّرٌ هنا — تمارا:** الإيداعُ في المحفظةِ يَجري لكلِّ طلبٍ مدفوعٍ غيرِ اشتراكيٍّ، ومنه المدفوعُ بالتقسيط. فعميلةٌ ألغت طلباً تماراويّاً تَحصلُ على رصيدٍ داخلَ التطبيقِ **وتَظلُّ مُلزَمةً بأقساطِ تمارا** لطرفٍ ثالث — وهذا يَختلفُ عن البطاقةِ اختلافاً نوعيّاً، لا درجةً. والبديلُ موجودٌ (`tamara.refund` في `tamara_api.js`)، لكنّ اختيارَه قرارٌ تجاريٌّ ومالِيٌّ لا إصلاحُ شفرة. النصُّ الآن صادقٌ في الحالتَين: يَقولُ «إلى محفظةِ زيارة لا إلى بطاقتكِ»، وهو ما يَجري فعلاً.

- **فشلُ مستمعٍ يُرسَمُ «لا شيء» — ومنه رقمُ ربحٍ مُضخَّمٌ بالرواتبِ كلِّها (2026-10-05).** القاعدةُ مكتوبةٌ في المستودعِ بخطِّ كاتبِها: `ScheduleBoard.tsx` يَقول «لا فشل صامت: بلا هذا تظهر الشاشة فارغة فيُفهم «لا مواعيد» خطأً»، و`Orders.tsx` يَقولُها عن مستمعِه الرئيس — **وبجوارِه في `useEffect` نفسِه مستمعانِ جسمُ مُعالِجِهما `console.error` وحدَه.** فالقاعدةُ عامّةٌ ومُنفَّذةٌ في بعضِ الصفحات، وهو نمطُ «قاعدةٌ عامّةٌ مُنفَّذةٌ في سطحٍ واحد» الذي تَكرّر هنا مرّاتٍ. مسحُ الثمانيةِ والعشرينَ مستمعاً أعطى أربعةَ عطبٍ حقيقيٍّ، كلٌّ يُنتجُ **دعوى** مكانَ «تعذّر القراءة»: **`Drivers.tsx`** — الصفحةُ بلا حالةِ تحميلٍ ولا خطأ، فجدولُ الأسطولِ يُرسَمُ فارغاً ويُقرأُ «لا سائقين» و«المتاحين حالياً» صفراً؛ **`Orders.tsx`** — حوارُ الإسنادِ يَطبعُ «لا يوجد سائقون متاحون حالياً» فيَمتنعُ الأدمنُ عن إسنادِ طلبٍ مدفوعٍ ولا يُخبَرُ بالسبب (والمستودعُ يَحملُ حادثةَ مُنتقٍ فارغٍ من قبل)؛ **`Support.tsx`** — خيطُ التذكرةِ يُقرأُ «لا توجد رسائل بعد. كن أول من يرد!» والعميلةُ تَنتظرُ رداً على رسالةٍ مكتوبةٍ لم تُقرأ، فيَرُدُّ الأدمنُ على غيرِ سياق؛ و**أخطرُها `Accountants.tsx`** — `drivers` تَبقى `[]` فـ`totalPayroll` صفرٌ و«صافي الأرباح» = الإيرادُ الصافي **كلُّه** بلا رواتب، **رقمُ مالٍ خاطئٌ في الاتجاهِ المتفائلِ بلا أيِّ علامة** بينما مستمعُ الإيرادِ ناجحٌ فالبطاقةُ تَبدو سليمة. الأرقامُ المشتقّةُ تُقرأُ «—» عند الجهل (قاعدةُ «لا رقمَ قبل أن نعرفه»)، والدعوى القديمةُ تَبقى صحيحةً في مكانِها بشرطِ أن **يَسبقَها** فرعُ الفشل — الترتيبُ هو الإصلاح.

  **ونتيجةٌ سالبةٌ تُسجَّلُ كما هي:** `Orders.tsx` عدّادُ «انتظار» صامتٌ كذلك و**ليس عطلاً** — الرسمُ `pendingCount ?? orders.filter(...).length` يَسقطُ إلى حسابٍ محلّيٍّ صحيحٍ من المستمعِ الرئيس، فالفشلُ لا يُنتجُ رقماً خاطئاً ولا دعوى؛ و`AdminNotificationsListener` صامتٌ **عن قصدٍ** بتعليقِه. وهما وحدَهما في قائمةِ `test/panel_listener_failure_test.dart` (٦ فحوص) المُعلَنةِ بأسبابِها، والفحصُ يُقارِنُ **مجموعةَ الصامتِ كاملةً** بها فمستمعٌ صامتٌ جديدٌ يُراجَعُ بدلَ أن يَمرّ. ومفتاحُ القائمةِ **ملفٌّ وترتيبٌ** لا رقمُ سطرٍ (كـ`stream_timeout_sweep_test`)، فتعديلٌ أعلى الملفِّ لا يُسقِطُ الحارسَ زوراً.

  **وثلاثةُ دروسٍ من كتابتِه، اثنانِ منها أخطائي:** (١) **«الحارسُ يَسقطُ على توثيقِه» في ثوبِ فحصِ ترتيبٍ لا مسحِ مصطلح** — تعليقي أعلى كلِّ صفحةٍ يَقتبسُ الدعوى نفسَها ليُبيّنَ العطل، فاقتباسُه يَسبقُ الفرعَ ويَقلبُ المقارنةَ؛ العلاجُ هو نفسُه (حجبُ أسطرِ التعليقِ ثمّ مُقابلةُ الخامّ) مطبَّقاً على `indexOf` لا على `contains`. (٢) **فحصُ «النجاحُ يُطفئُ العَلَم» كان يُرضيه زرُّ الإعادة**: صياغتُه الأولى طلبَت `set*Error(false)` في أيِّ موضعٍ من الملفِّ، و`onClick` الزرِّ يَحملُه — فمرَّ اختبارُ قضمٍ أزالَ الإطفاءَ من مسارِ النجاحِ **أخضرَ**، أي أنّ خطأً عابراً يَلتصقُ بالشاشةِ حتى يَضغطَ الأدمنُ الإعادةَ؛ الفحصُ مشدودٌ الآن إلى **وسيطِ النجاحِ بعينِه**. (٣) **الفاصلةُ المتدلّيةُ تُنتجُ وسيطاً أخيرَ فارغاً**، فيُقرأُ مُعالِجُ خطأٍ «صامتٌ» حيث لا مُعالِجَ أصلاً — وقد صادفَ أنّ تلك الحالةَ صامتةٌ فعلاً، أي أنّ النتيجةَ كانت صحيحةً **بالمصادفة**؛ الاستخراجُ يُسقِطُ الفارغَ، وفحصٌ يُثبِتُ أنّه أصابَ نصَّ ذلك المُعالِجِ بعينِه (`صامت عند الخطأ`) فلا يَتنكّرُ العُقمُ في صورةِ «صامتٌ مسموحٌ به». ستّةُ اختباراتِ قضمٍ، كلٌّ وحدَه.

- **ونظيرُ الدارت للعطلِ نفسِه، في اللحظةِ التي صارَ فيها قابلَ الوصول (2026-10-05).** `firstEventTimeout` حوّلَ التعليقَ الأبديَّ إلى **خطأ**، فصارت فروعُ `hasError` مَسلكاً حقيقيّاً لا نظريّاً — ومَن لا يَفحصُها يَسقطُ إلى فرعِ «لا بيانات»، فتُقرأُ النتيجةُ دعوى. مسحُ ٥٨ `StreamBuilder` أعطى خمسةً: **`client_dashboard`** (الملفُّ بأكمله **صفرُ ذكرٍ** لـ`hasError`) — بطاقةُ «طلبكِ الجاري» وبطاقةُ «باقتي» تَسقطانِ إلى `SizedBox.shrink()`، فعميلةٌ لها طلبٌ مدفوعٌ مُسنَدٌ أو باقةٌ ساريةٌ تَفتحُ الرئيسيّةَ فلا تَرى أثراً له فتَقرأُ غيابَه «لا طلبَ لي»؛ **`map_screen`** (خريطةُ تتبّعِ الإدارة) — `data` فارغةٌ فتُقرأُ البطاقةُ «جاري التعيين...» بحالةِ `pending` ويُخفى المبلغ، أي أنّ الإدارةَ تَستنتجُ أنّ الطلبَ بلا سائقٍ وهو مُسنَد؛ **`admin_managers_screen`** — `!hasData → CircularProgressIndicator` وحدَه، و`hasData` كاذبٌ على الخطأ، **فدوّارةٌ لا تَنتهي**؛ و**`admin_broadcast_screen`** — قسمُ «البث السابق» يَسقطُ بصمتٍ فيُقرأُ «لا سجلّ». و**القاعدةُ كانت مُنفَّذةً في ثلاثةٍ من هذه الملفّاتِ نفسِها** بتعليقٍ يَشرحُها («فشل البث كان يُعرض كقائمة فارغة — خطأ صريح مع إعادة محاولة») على بُعدِ أسطرٍ من المستمعِ الصامت.

  **والسوابقُ المسموحةُ ثمانٍ، كلٌّ بسببِه**: بوّابةُ الصيانةِ (fail-open موثَّق)، وشريطُ العروضِ وشارةُ عدّادِ الإشعاراتِ (غيابُ بانرٍ أو شارةٍ ليس جملةً)، وثلاثةُ `StreamBuilder<Duration>` على مؤقّتٍ محلّيٍّ لا شبكةَ فيه، وشاشتا الشروطِ والخصوصيّةِ (تَسقطانِ على النصِّ الثابتِ بقرارٍ موثَّق). `test/stream_error_branch_test.dart` (٦ فحوص) يُقارِنُ **مجموعةَ** الصامتِ كاملةً بتلك القائمةِ بمفتاحِ ملفٍّ وترتيبٍ لا رقمِ سطر، ويَشدُّ أنّ فرعَ الخطأِ **يَسبقُ** فرعَ «لا بيانات» (الترتيبُ هو الإصلاح: `!hasData` صحيحٌ على الخطأِ أيضاً، فلو سبقَ لَأَكلَ الفرعَ الجديدَ وبقي العطلُ بزينةٍ فوقَه)، وأنّ `firstEventTimeout` ما زالت على البثوثِ المُصلَحةِ (بلاها لا يَصِلُ `hasError` قطّ فيَصيرُ كلُّ ما أعلاه زينةً)، وأنّ الفروعَ السابقةَ في تلك الملفّاتِ لم تُنقَض.

  **وتصحيحانِ لنفسي، كلاهما في المُحلِّلِ لا في الشفرة.** أوّلُ مسحٍ أعلنَ `admin_orders_screen` صامتاً — وفرعُه موجودٌ في `_buildBody` — لأنّ المُحلِّلَ لم يَتبع الإحالة؛ وأوّلُ تتبّعٍ للإحالةِ أخذَ **أوّلَ `{`** بعد اسمِ الدالّةِ فالتقطَ قوسَ المعامَلاتِ المُسمّاة `{required bool canLoadMore}` لا جسمَها — **فخُّ الحدِّ غيرِ المُوازَنِ سادسَ مرّة** في هذا المستودع. والتتبّعُ يُوازنُ قائمةَ المعامَلاتِ أوّلاً، ويَستثني كلماتَ دارت المحجوزةَ (`if (… snapshot …)` كان يُقرأُ نداءَ دالّةٍ اسمُها `if` فيُعلِنُ موضعاً «مُعالَجاً» زوراً)، وفحصٌ يُثبِتُ أنّ الإحالتَين المعروفتَين تُقرأانِ فعلاً. وثالثةٌ: حارسُ تأنيثِ الخطابِ عضَّ على نصّي الجديد — `map_screen` خارجَ `admin/` فيَشمَلُه مسحُ شاشاتِ العميلةِ، وفاتحُه الوحيدُ اليومَ شاشةُ الإدارة؛ فالجملةُ صارت **بلا جنسٍ** بدلَ توسيعِ استثناءاتِ الحارس، وهو قرارُ الحارسِ نفسِه فيما يَقرؤه أكثرُ من دور. ستّةُ اختباراتِ قضمٍ، كلٌّ وحدَه.

- **ومسحُ النمطِ أكمل: `zone_locator_test` كان ثلاثَ شاشاتٍ والمُحدِّدُ في ثمانٍ — والشفرةُ سليمةٌ كلُّها.** Four assertions in that guard are general ("no screen calls `Geolocator.` directly", "all use the shared service and card", "none shows one message for every failure", "the locate button asks for permission explicitly") and ran over a hand-written list of three. The other five screens that locate a zone (`car_interior`, `event_worker_packages`, `store_schedule`, `subscription_plans`, plus the picker which is excluded by role) were checked one by one and **satisfy every assertion already** — so the fault was in the guard's list alone, not in the code. The list is now *derived* from the consumers (every `lib/screens/*.dart` naming `ZyiarahZoneLocator.locate`, minus the picker), with a check that the derivation finds at least seven so it cannot silently become empty — an inert guard being worse than none. A new screen that locates a zone now enters the scope by itself. **And a negative result worth recording:** `pre_vat_display_test` looked under-scoped too (five selection screens) but is not — the store cart says «المجموع قبل الضريبة» while the guard requires the literal «الإجمالي قبل الضريبة»; both are correct Arabic, the cart's own claim is covered by `vat_display_claim_test`'s sweep of all client screens, and adding it would have meant relaxing a guard's literal to make a file pass. It was tried, it failed for that reason, and the addition was reverted rather than the guard loosened.

- **مراجعةُ ما أُنجز، وخللٌ أدخلَه إصلاحٌ: الصفرُ أطفأ الفحصَين (2026-10-05).** Re-reading the wallet price-verification block of the previous batch found a defect **the fix itself introduced**. Subtracting the trusted discount — the right change, it stopped a >50% coupon from flagging an honest payment and a >80% one from being refused — made `pkgExpectedGross = Math.max(0, expected - trusted)` reach **`0`** when a coupon covers the full price. The two conditions around it were untouched and read `if (pkgExpectedGross && amount < …)`, so a zero expected is **falsy** and *both* the rejection and the `price_mismatch` flag turn off together: a wallet payment of any amount against a fully-discounted order passes with nothing written. Before the discount was subtracted, `expected` came from `grossFromBaseRounded` on a `base > 0` and could never be zero, so the condition was sound where it stood — the shape is `amounts.js`' «explicit `0` is a decision, not an absence», one floor down. The Moyasar path already names this state: `const suspiciousZero = expectedNet <= 0 && paid > 0;` and folds it into Tier A (`ratio < 0.5 || suspiciousZero`). The wallet path now distinguishes `verifiable = pkgExpectedGross !== null` (the real "couldn't check") from the zero, rejects only when `> 0` and below 20%, and flags on `suspiciousZero` or below 50%. **It deliberately does not reject on `suspiciousZero`**, and the asymmetry is the point: Moyasar's Tier-B enforcement floor is `expectedNet >= 5`, which excludes the ~100%-coupon case from being refused at all, so rejecting here would reinstate the very defect — a valid payment refused — that subtracting the discount had just removed, from the other direction. Checks (١٦) and (١٧) in `functions/test/price_verify.test.js` pin it: the falsy-zero condition is gone from the code while still quoted in the comment that explains its removal (the counter-assertion, for the eighth time this session), and the `throw` stays **ahead** of the `suspiciousZero ||` branch so the zero can never reach the rejection. Both were bite-tested one at a time — restoring the old condition fails only (١٦), moving `suspiciousZero` into the `throw` fails only (١٧).

- **فهرسٌ لمجموعةٍ واحدةٍ من طابورَين — وشبكةُ الأمانِ ماتت للطابورَين (2026-10-05).** Reviewing the notification-queue split of the previous batch. `opsHealthSweep`'s redrive — the net written after the August outage left 122 welcome notifications unprocessed — went from scanning one collection to looping two, and the query is compound: `processed == false`, `createdAt <= now-30m`, `orderBy createdAt`. **Firestore composite indexes are per collection, not shared**, and `firestore.indexes.json` carried that index for `notification_triggers` only. So the `notification_queue` query — the collection where *every* `queuePush` notification now lives — fails with `FAILED_PRECONDITION`. And it fails **first**, because the loop names it first and **one `try` wrapped the whole loop**: the throw aborts the iteration before `notification_triggers` is ever read, so a stalled client-written trigger stops being redriven too. Net: the net is gone for both queues and all that is left is one `console.error` line. Two fixes, because the index alone would leave the next failure just as total: the index is added, and the `try` moves **inside** the loop, naming its collection in the error. A third site rode along: `functions/diagnose_email.js` — the script you run **when email is broken** — still read `notification_triggers` alone, so it would report «0 pending» over a backed-up server queue; it loops both now. `test/firestore_indexes_guard_test.dart` ties both indexes to the loop in both directions (an index outliving its query is write cost for nothing; a query without its index is worse), pins that the `try` sits inside the loop before the query, and pins the diagnostic. **A sweep afterwards found no other missing index** — 13 compound queries across `lib/`, `functions/` and the panel, all served — and the reason this one escaped is worth keeping: the collection name is a **loop variable**, so no name-based sweep can see it, which is why the guard pins the literal array rather than trusting a sweep. One bite test also failed to bite at first, for the eighth time this session: the diagnostic check passed over a reverted file because the *comment* explaining the fix still names `notification_queue` — strip comment lines, then counter-assert the word is still in the raw text.

- **تحقّقُ السعرِ الخادميُّ كان على مسلكَين من عشرةٍ إلى `is_paid` — والمسلكانِ هما اللذانِ يَختارُ العميلُ نداءَهما (2026-10-05).** The field `amount` on an order is written by the client's device; `functions/pricing.js` recomputing the base from the trusted zone document is the only thing that catches an under-declared price. CLAUDE.md said that recompute runs "on every Moyasar/wallet payment" — true of the two `onCall` functions and of nothing else. A sweep of every site that writes `is_paid: true` found **ten**, and the recompute on two. The two gaps were live: **`moyasarWebhook`** has no recompute at all — its only money check is `verifiedPayment.amount === expectedAmount(data)`, and `expectedAmount` is `final_amount ?? total_amount ?? planPrice ?? amount`, **every field of which the client writes at creation**, so the comparison is "paid equals declared", not "declared equals the zone's price": declare one riyal, pay one riyal, pass. And **`_tamaraFlipPaid`** (the Tamara webhook *and* the `confirmPendingTamaraOrders` sweep) has no amount check whatsoever. Worse, `verifyMoyasarPayment` returns early on `is_paid === true` **before** its own check, so a webhook that lands first — and it often does, since Moyasar fires it server-to-server the instant the payment settles while the callable waits for the app to come back from the payment sheet — **consumes the whole check**; a tamperer simply never calls it. Net: `price_mismatch`, the admin alert, and Tier B enforcement were all skippable by not making one client call. The ~85-line block is now one function, `_verifyOrderPriceTierA(db, orderRef, orderId, od, paid, source)`, called from all three paths — the extraction was checked to be behaviour-preserving by normalising whitespace and comparing the two bodies, which came out identical. `source` tags every log line, so `[price-shadow:webhook]` is distinguishable from `[price-shadow:verify]`. **Tier B was deliberately not widened**, and the asymmetry is the decision rather than an oversight: Tier A marks and alerts with no financial consequence, which is exactly the wallet path's accepted policy ("marks and leans on the periodic sweep"), while Tier B voids or refunds a customer's card with no human in the loop — and the evidence that enabled it ("zero alarms throughout the monitoring period", owner, 2026-07-31) was gathered on the callable path, which, if the webhook was routinely winning the race, **never saw this population at all**. So the webhook logs `TIER_B_WOULD_BLOCK` and the guard pins that enforcement stays single-pathed until the owner decides on the evidence; widening it is one line. Two more things fell out: the comment above the check still claimed Tier B was off (`ENFORCE_PRICE_TIER_B = true` since 2026-07-31, stated correctly at the top of the file and wrongly 2,500 lines down), and `payContractWithWallet` turned out to be a tenth `is_paid` site I had missed — **the guard found it, not me**, because check (٢٢) compares the *whole set* of enclosing function names against a pinned list with a reason beside each. It is correctly out of scope (`planPrice` comes from the contract document, and a fixed-price contract skips base/terrain/surge). Six new checks in `functions/test/price_verify.test.js` (23 total), each bite-tested alone. And `amounts.test.js` caught a mistake of mine that no human review would: the new function's **doc comment quotes the amount chain**, so the "no inline chain outside the module" guard failed on my documentation — the ninth instance this session. It strips comment lines now and counter-asserts; and the Tamara site, which I had written as a two-field hand-made chain, calls `amounts.expectedAmount` instead, since its question is that module's question verbatim.

- **«حارسٌ ضيّقٌ وقاعدةٌ عامّة» مرّتَينِ أُخريَين — والمؤنّثُ أنتجَ جملةً بجنسَين (2026-10-05).** Reviewing the feminine-address batch. Its pattern list was **hand-picked** (eight verbs), so every imperative outside it survived — and two of the survivors sit **in the same sentence as a feminine verb**: the login screen's «محاولات كثيرة — **انتظر** قليلاً ثم **أعيدي** المحاولة», and the payment dialog's «إن كنت قد دُفعت فلا **تقلق** — … أو **تواصلي** مع الدعم». A second sweep with a wider list (thirteen more verbs plus the adjectives) found **19 client-facing sites**. The payment dialog's wording was wrong in a second way the first pass did not look for: «إن كنتِ قد دُفعت» says *you* were paid, not *the amount was deducted* — on the one dialog she sees when a payment fails to complete, carrying the reference number. It now reads «إن كان المبلغُ قد خُصم فلا تقلقي». **Four strings were made genderless rather than feminine, and the distinction is the decision:** `global_error_handler` is shown to all three roles, `location_picker_screen`'s search field is opened by `admin_hourly_zones_screen` too, the home-notes example addresses the *crew*, and the referral text is what **she sends to other people** («أهلك وأصدقائك») — so that one became plural, and lost a word of dialect («وبتحصل») it had been carrying. Three `سجل` hits are nouns («سجل الطلبات»، «سجل تجاري»، «سجل آخر المعاملات») and stay, so the new pattern matches the shadda'd imperative plus «سجل الدخول» explicitly and is bite-tested against the noun. **And the raw-exception rule was narrow a third time:** it iterates `lib/screens/**`, while `lib/widgets/` holds two dialogs the client opens from her own screens — `rating_dialog` showed «تعذّر فتح الكاميرا — … من الإعدادات: $e» and `support_fab` «تعذّر الاتصال بالدعم: $e». A sweep of `widgets`+`utils`+`providers`+`models` found exactly those two and no admin-facing site needing an exemption, so the scope is now all of `lib/widgets/`. Two familiar consequences followed, both fixed rather than silenced: `offers_screen_coupons_test` pinned a rewritten literal **for the second time** and was repointed, and removing `$e` made both snackbars constant so `prefer_const_constructors` fired. The guard now also pins the four genderless strings, so a later "finish the job" pass cannot feminise what more than one role reads — a bite test confirms feminising `global_error_handler` fails.

- **تعطيلُ سائقٍ ثلاثةُ حقولٍ، وقد أُصلح مرّةً في موضعٍ واحدٍ من ثلاثة — `lib/utils/driver_activation.dart` + `admin_panel/src/utils/driverActivation.ts` (2026-10-05).** Three fields carry a driver's disabled state and they are read by three different consumers: the server's assignment rule reads **`is_active`** alone (`isAssignableDriver`), `unassignJobsOnDriverDisable` reads **either** (`is_active === false || is_suspended === true`), and the web panel builds its **status badge and its stop/activate button** on **`is_suspended`**, while `is_available` is the driver's own connection state (his app sets it on open, the server sets it when a job completes). **The defect was already found once and fixed at one site:** `admin_compliance_screen`'s ban carries a comment saying it in as many words — «لوحة الويب تبني شارة الحالة ومفتاح الإيقاف على `is_suspended`، فحظرٌ يكتب `is_active` فقط كان يُظهر السائق «نشطاً» في الويب رغم تعطيله هنا» — and the **main** toggle, in the drivers screen itself, still wrote `{'is_active': val}` alone. So disabling a driver from the Flutter admin app left the panel showing him «متاح» with a green pulsing dot, its button offering «إيقاف» as though he were still active, and the dashboard's «السائقون المتاحون» tile counting him. The money path was never wrong — the server refuses the assignment with «السائق غير نشط» — so this is a **picture-and-count** defect: the admin plans the day on capacity that does not exist. The fields now live once per side, and **enable deliberately does not write `is_available`**: being online is the driver's decision, and claiming it because an admin re-enabled him would be a new lie in the same place. The panel's badge, its button label **and the button's own decision** now go through `driverIsDisabled` (reading either field, like the server) — the last one mattered because «إيقاف» on an already-`is_active: false` driver would disable him again. The dashboard filters **locally** rather than adding a composite index, per the repo's standing rule. `test/driver_activation_test.dart` (10 checks) pins the two maps, their cross-language equality, every one of the three writers, the panel's three reads, and — the part that keeps the mirror honest — that **the server still reads the fields in that exact shape**, since the helper is only correct as long as it is. Three bite tests, one per half.

- **عَلَمٌ يَكتبُه العميلُ عند الإنشاء يُسكِتُ التنبيهَ الوحيدَ لمسارِ المحفظة (2026-10-05).** The STAGE-C order-create rule blocks eight server-trust fields by name; the rest of the server's flags were open, and **one of them mutes the only alert the wallet underpayment path has**. `opsHealthSweep` alerts through `alertBatch(docs, "ops_alerted_mismatch", …)`, whose first line is `docs.filter(({d}) => d[flag] !== true)` — the flag means "already alerted". The Tier A path sets it *because it alerts immediately* and says so in its own comment; **the wallet path marks `price_mismatch` silently and leans on that sweep alone** («مسار المحفظة يَسِم بصمت فيغطيه الفحص الدوري»). So a client who creates her order with `ops_alerted_mismatch: true` and then pays from the wallet below half the expected price but above a fifth of it — the band that is marked, not rejected — is **served at an underpaid price and nobody is ever told**. Three more were open in the same way: `tamper_handled` is Tier B's claim (pre-set, `voidOrRefundTampered` returns `"already"` without calling the gateway at all), `final_amount` leads the whole amount chain in `amounts.js`, and `auto_refund_processed` stops the unfulfilled-order refund. Sixteen names are added to the blocklist — the four `ops_alerted_*` of the `alertBatch` family, the two tamper claims, the five price marks, the three refund-sweep flags, `coupon_rejected_reason` and `final_amount`. **Nothing legitimate writes any of them:** zero writes in `lib/` for all fifteen flags, and `final_amount` is 14 reads and no write there, 12 reads and no write server-side. Five emulator checks cover it, and the **bite test proves the hole was real rather than theoretical**: with the original eight-name list all four forged creates **succeed**, while the check that a normal client create still passes (coupon, discount, zone, hours, booking fields) stays green either way. As with the `referral_code` fix, **this does nothing in production until a human deploys the rules** — automation never publishes `firestore.rules`, and this rule is the one carrying the STAGE-C hold.

- **سلّةُ المتجرِ لم تكن تُعادُ تسعيرُها خادميّاً إطلاقاً — `resolveStoreCartBase` (2026-10-05).** `store_service.dart` *does* price the cart from `products` inside a Firestore transaction: it reads each `products/{id}`, takes the server's `price`, and writes `total_amount = grossFromBaseRounded(sum)` plus `items` carrying `{id, name, quantity, price}`. But it does that **in the client**, and its own variable is called `serverCalculatedTotal`, which is exactly the wrong name for it. A tamperer writing the `store_orders` document straight from the SDK declares whatever they like: the rule requires `is_paid: false` and a matching `client_id` and **does not look at the amount**, and `moyasarWebhook`'s only money check is "paid equals declared" through `expectedAmount`, every field of which the same writer controls. So a five-hundred-riyal cart is declared at one riyal, paid at one riyal, and shipped. And there was **no server-side price verification for the store of any kind**: `computeExpectedBasePrice` returns `null` for it (its items are not a service in a zone), so the whole Tier A chain is scoped to `orders` — a gap the earlier «مسلكَين من عشرة» finding did not cover, because that one was about *which paths* run the check, and this is about a collection the check was never written for.
